It is an environmental problem that questions the policies for the promotion of shellfish and the policy for the sanitation and purification of waters, which has very obvious consequences for thousands of families living in the shellfish, the marine banks, and which, in addition, poses problems for the whole of the coastal population and for the entire population of Galicia. In the last legislation in which you are president, you produce only 7,100 annual averages. So there's been a 25% drop in production, a drop that you're somehow responsible for. Look, Your Honor, I'm going to give you the data: 1,110 million euros have been invested in water purification and sanitation since 2009. We built 45 debuggers, which serve 1,700,000 Greeks; i.e. we built debuggers for more than half of the population and we did performances in 165 municipalities; i.e., more than half of Galician municipalities. If you add up the number of works, you will give a division: five sanitation performances every month, since the year 2009; and since the year 2009, the water output is that 70% were of good quality and now we're at 77%. It's not only improved water quality, it's improved marine activity, and the data I'm going to give you are data from the ranges, it's not data from the opinion of different people. From April of the year 200 9 to September of the year 2017 there were, for example, 43 areas of activity in which we solved their situation, improving 1,400 kilometers of sea-banks to more than 1,650 bats, which is, by the way, 50% of the total Galician bats. I want to give you, without the courage to come into discussion, Mr. Leicega, the number of seafood banks you've healed in four years: 5; we, 43. But we keep giving data. In 2010, 8 million square meters of areas recovered for shellfish, and what is important — and I think it's a fact that you, who is an economist, should value —: the shellfish has increased in their discharges by 13% and in their billing by 18% since 2013. I repeat the data again: 13% of the downloads since 2013 and 38% of the billing. The Bass Fleet: Downloads, an increase of 24%, 45% by billing. On the permex — the permits — 2015 was the first year of the historic series initiated in which the permex 2,000 people, from shellfish on foot, and since 2014 we had a positive balance in the number of permex. This is the data, Mr. Leicega. So we keep improving the quality of the water, five works a month. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-10-18-DSPG045.u143	Thank you, Mr. President. We continue with point 4 of the agenda, non-legislative proposals. G.P.'s non-legislative proposal. People of Galicia, at the initiative of Mr José González Vázquez and five other Members, on the request by the Government of Greece to the central government of the transfer to autonomous communities of the performance of succession tax and grants arising from the real obligation to contribute, the personal obligation of the non-resident cause, as well as the acquisition of immovable property outside Spain, together with the delegation of the corresponding management, collection, inspection and review powers
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-10-18-DSPG045.u144	No amendments were tabled. Mr. González Vázquez has the floor to make the non-law proposal.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-10-18-DSPG045.u145	Good morning, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen, today we are debating a non-law proposal on taxation for inheritance tax and donations of non-residents. I wanted to say, first of all, that in such matters it is mandatory to use technical arguments rigorously to avoid generating false expectations in affected citizens. I have been insisting, whenever I talk about tax issues, in this respect, both with the taxation of returned migrants and with respect to municipal surpluses. Today we are dealing with an issue which is true that it is of great importance in Galicia, given the large number of migrants we have in our community. Good evidence of this importance is that in the last three years alone, taxation on this non-resident tax has brought 15 million euros. To understand, when we talk about non-residents, we're talking about our emigrants. I want, in order to contextualize our initiative, to speak in advance of the tax on successions and prayers. It is a tax that records free transmissions, whether it is between living people — they would be prayers — and in the event of the death of the cause — there would be successions. Many of you may even know it as real rights. In the configuration of our tax system, it's a tax that is taxed; that is, it's not taxed by autonomous communities, it's a tribute from the state, but in which autonomous communities have regulatory powers. Ladies and gentlemen, this is a measure of self-governance, which promotes co-responsibility between the state and the autonomous communities and the financial autonomy of the autonomous communities. I mean, autonomous communities can make their own decisions about this tax. This regulatory competition which the Autonomous Community has allowed the Union to regulate a significant reduction in taxation for taxpayers — and this is important — residents of Galicia. As of January 1, 2016, a reform was carried out which led to the de facto abolition of the tax for 99% of the Greeks and the Greeks. And without demagogic arguments, it's not a tax discount to benefit the rich, it's a tax discount to the common people. Starting January 1, 2016, they only pay the inheritance tax for those who have a high estate. Since that date, we have exempted from the tax payment to all persons who receive an inheritance from an ascendant, descendant or spouse, when the part that receives each heir is less than EUR 400,000. I mean, from 0 to 400,000 euros in an inheritance today in Galicia, no inheritance tax is paid. This change places Galicia among the autonomous communities with a lower taxation on inheritance transmission — to be noted — in cases of smaller estates. A child who today receives an inheritance of EUR 400,000, ladies and gentlemen, pays in Galicia zero euros of taxes, and more than EUR 60,000 in communities like Andalusia or Asturias, which have the same capacity and could change their regulations. In addition, this is a particularly beneficial reform in an autonomous community where the possibility, for our civil right, of the inheritances in life: covenants of improvement or departure. In conceptual terms, why do we make this reduction? To benefit people. Look, among other things, we foster the savings of older people, we favor economic recovery because we allow those who receive inheritance to have more money. In 2016 alone there were over 62,000 Greeks and Greeks who inherited without paying taxes; they saved 41 million euros. We also seek to maintain integrity to the family heritage and the economic capacity of the home. And we're looking, gentlemen, to avoid a problem for me hard to understand and that it intensified with the economic crisis, and it was the fact that many children or grandchildren had to give up their inheritances because they couldn't pay the tax. This is a certain fact. Since we made this change, the waivers to inheritances in this autonomous community have been drastically reduced. However — although I repeat that it is a tax handed down — the tax is not entirely handed over to the Autonomous Community. As pointed out in Article 32 of Law 22/2009 — which regulates the system of financing of autonomous communities — the transfer is conditional on the fact that the taxable persons or property are located in Spanish territory. So — and here is the problem we bring to the House today — the tax is not passed on in the following cases — the following: in the case of succession tax, when the deceased, the cause, is non-resident or when what the inheritance receives is non-resident. In the case of the donation tax, when it comes to immovable property located outside Spanish territory or when it comes to a person who receives the donation and does not live in Spain. Until 1 January 2015 — and now we'll talk about why this change is taking place — in these assumptions the performance did not correspond to the autonomous community of Galicia, it corresponded to the state. And in addition, in these assumptions, the state was responsible for management, inspection and collection. What was the consequence? That was applicable to state legislation. That is, when there was an inheritance of an emigrant, it applied state legislation, which is more serious for its heirs than the current legislation of the Autonomous Community of Greece. This led to situations of clear discrimination. Simply by giving a data, a father who died in Galicia, with an estate in our autonomous community of 300,000 euros, with a son who lives in Galicia and another who lives in Germany, if he left half the inheritance to each one, it could happen that Galicia’s — and in fact happened — had not paid the inheritance tax and, however, that of Germany had to pay according to state rules about 20,000 euros of tax. That is to say, we are talking about a clear discrimination which led to a judgment by the Court of Justice of the European Union of 3 September 2014, which declared that the Kingdom of Spain in this field was in breach of its obligations to the table, both the Treaty establishing the European Union and the Agreement on European Economic Area of 2 May 1992. So what you're going to say to the judgment is that you can't discriminate against residents or non-residents. What is the consequence of the Judgment? On the one hand, it eliminates this tax regime, which is technically called "by real obligation", but at the same time it supports, allows and says that according to Community legislation there is a plurality of tax regimes within the Spanish State in the matter of successions and prayers. I mean, what the Court of Justice of the European Union tells us is that autonomous communities can continue to decide to have different systems in succession tax. And there may be autonomous communities like the one that benefits its citizens with a tax close to zero in 99% of the cases, and others, such as Andalusia or how it can now be Asturias, who record much more inheritances. The Spanish lawmakers had to adapt their legislation, and here they had two possibilities. The first possibility was to unify the whole tax of successions and prayers — I say among all the autonomous communities —. The second was to amend the rules governing the applicable legislation. And this is what was done, through a modification — which is collected in Law 26/2014 — with effect from January 1, 2015, what you said is that in these cases of non-residents the legislation of autonomous communities had been applied, although management continued in the hands of the State. With this regulation, therefore, the situations of non-residents — I can collect all the different areas — for example, successions with cause not resident, i.e., Greek deceased in Germany with all its heritage in Germany, and its heirs reside in Galicia, would tax in accordance with Greek law. Also in cases of succession with non-resident heir. It would be the case of a person who lives in Galicia and dies leaving, for example, heirs in Germany. Tribute for goods situated in Spain in accordance with the Italian regulations. And, I repeat, it is more beneficial than state legislation, which was the one that applied before this reform. Donations of immovable property in Spain in favor of non-residents. For example, a flat located in Ourense and a child living in Germany, also taxes according to German law. That is to say, a change is made — which is good for us — of adapting to the judgment of the Court of Justice. But we understand that we have to go further, because, if it is true that the legislation of the autonomous communities applies, tax management — in this case of non-residents — continues to correspond to the state, and for our parliamentary group the most convenient solution would have been the full assignment of this tax to the autonomous communities, incorporating non-residents according to the points of connection that are collected in national legislation. In this way, we seek to achieve a homogeneous and equal application of the rule to all taxpayers by tax administrations specialized in this tribute; to achieve greater efficiency in management; to facilitate the obligations of taxpayers, significantly reducing indirect tax pressure; and also — and this is important — that in cases that have to be taxed because it is marked by autonomous legislation, these incomes go directly to the autonomous communities. I'm done now, ladies and gentlemen, saying that this is the content of our resolution. We asked for the full tax assignment in the case of successions of non-residents, and we asked for this assignment as a formula for the best defense of the interests of these Greeks and Greeks who have ties to the outside. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-10-18-DSPG045.u206	Thank you, President. Good morning to all and to all. A report by the European Environment Agency warned of the consequences and risks that climate change had for Galicia: from rising sea level to rising average temperature, but above all by falling precipitation, which could soon reach up to 30% less in many months. In spite of this, Galicia continues to have the largest CO2 outbreaks in the state, and our greenhouse gas emissions account for 0.12% of the world's total, when we only reach 0.045% of the total population. The Galician Union has done nothing to avoid this situation, approving in many policy cases that go contrary. Therefore, their policies have contributed to the scarcity of water in recent days, which affects the proper extinction of forest fires, but also in a very significant way to the supply of drinking water in many municipalities of Galicia. The Union simply looks at the sky to see what happens, without any proposals to improve this situation. The old conception of our country as surplus in water reserves, where they were unimaginable drought periods like those we are suffering in recent years, has acted on public policies, so that neither their conservation nor proper use of water has been promoted, occupying only the Galician Board of Emergency Plans in the face of this situation, forgetting again the necessary prevention policies before the warnings and indicators that have been warning that this situation has been approaching for decades. Today, with this LNP, from our group, we want to focus on a very specific issue, a very specific situation, which is worrying and is being totally neglected by the German Government. Galicia, with Asturias, presents the worst efficiency rates of the water supply network in the state, with rates below 60%, and very close to 50% in most cases. This means that up to 50% of our water reserves are lost during transport from the uptake to the homes or industries, which is very far from the best reachable 80%. For years this was not a problem, because, except for exceptional issues, supply is guaranteed by the conditions of our country, but today there is a need for corrective measures to improve these indices. By this situation, and referring specifically to the efficiency of water channel management, we present this initiative, where we urge the Galician Union to establish a plan to improve the water distribution network throughout the country, in collaboration with the municipalities and the Fegamp, which includes, among other things, the following actions: renewal of networks and counters; use of flow management applications in real time; development of mechanisms and flight control equipment; reduction of unregistered consumption; implementation of surveillance systems in the distribution network with pre-location; establishment of periodic plans for inspection and control of distribution networks; communication campaigns against fraud and proper use of the network; plans to review suspicious consumption; and, lastly, periodic evaluations of the plan's result. These are our concrete proposals on this plan, which we believe must contain the possible solution to this problem and improve this situation, but of course we are open to any other proposal. We want to urge the Council of Galicia to set up a plan. If technicians consider that some of these proposals can be improved, deleted or replaced, as welcome. But what we think is essential is to have a plan. The scarcity of water is aggravated by having inefficient channels, but ultimately the real problem, the reason why we reach this situation and it will not be resolved by improving the effectiveness and effectiveness of channels, it has to do with environmental aggressions and the contribution mainly to climate change. Those who do not seem to care about anyone, because, still suffering in such an obvious way already the consequences, it seems that no one is in a hurry to take the commitments needed to try to reverse this situation — if it is still possible to turn it back at this point — but at least to try to stop its progress and to handle it in some way. The problem of climate change is a problem that is facing cross-cutting policies, and in Galicia it's not even facing itself since the Environmental Council. All we have to do is look at the waste management model, the Sogama model, where the environmental advisor considers an international example of waste management which only recycling 3.5 % of all waste managed and which leads to the incineration of most waste. Obviously, incineration eliminates CO2 from other pollutants, which contributes to climate change. But there are a lot of policies that need to change, as is the forest. This fear of the People's Party of converting Galicia into a huge green carpet of eucalyptuses, and, beyond that, into a huge carpet of foreign and alocon trees. In Galicia it is the paradox that we have reversed the percentages of foreign plants and native plants. When it's normal in most places, it's that autochthonous people are above 80%, in Galicia, the outsides are the ones that approach those numbers. With regard to drought, the eucalyptus is a plant that, although Mrs. Mato doesn't know, besides pyrophila, is a devastating one — I say she doesn't know because she said it on the radio this morning — an annihilator of water resources. So the more eucalyptus we plant in Galicia, the more drought problems we will have and the less water resources we will have. So there are policies that are not compatible. We cannot attempt to solve the problem of climate change by incinerating solid waste. You cannot attempt to solve the water resource problem by planting more eucalyptus. That is why I say that the necessary transversality in environmental policies. In mobility policies, it is no less the People's Party's contribution to climate change, which still continues to defend as the main policy in infrastructure and mobility that every Greek and Greek has a high-capacity track less than 15 minutes from their home. I don't know how you intend to fight climate change by betting on individual motor transport and fossil fuel consumption. It's obviously not compatible, or something, or something else. Either bet on sustainable mobility, or bet on high-capacity channels and that every galego and galega has a high-capacity track less than 15 minutes. They can explain to you how these two things can be compatible or, if not, they're not doing anything to fight climate change. Also the People's Party's crusade against the energy transition, in its bet on getting on the side of the electric oligopolie, is another of the great problems we face in the fight against climate change. You can't fight the energy transition, put sun taxes, avoid self-consumption, and say that you're fighting climate change, because it's not compatible with one another. So, either you're doing one thing, or you're doing the other. They're either fighting climate change, or they're fueling climate change, which is what we're saying; and I'm setting very specific and very clear examples about it. The director of Galician Waters recently made statements calling on citizens to reduce water consumption. People's Party proposals are always to throw balloons out. Obviously, we have to raise awareness of citizenship so that we can better manage this resource. It's already too late for you to carry out serious awareness campaigns so that citizenship tries to consume as little as possible, but the reality is that domestic consumption is a very small part of total consumption, therefore, only focusing on that issue will not be resolved. We agree with Mr. Roberto Rodríguez, who makes a call to citizenship so you didn't waste the resource, but we told Mr. Roberto Rodríguez that wasting the resource is also that half of the water coming from the catchment areas is lost on the way. So that's where we can do more and that's where the Galician Union has to get to work on this issue. For me, that's all.

And when I talk about educational communities, of course, I talk about teachers, but I also talk about -- like I don't! -- mothers and parents of students and the same students. It is true that, on a purely ideological issue, within this pernicious and unnecessary modification of the organic law of education, aspects have been introduced that diminish, when they do not directly eliminate, the participation of that educational community in decisions that are extremely important to the reality of our centres. The first area I'd like to highlight, because that's how they are sequenced in our non-law proposition, is what we mean by planning in the organization of the network of centers and, therefore, of educational concerts. And here it is not only that it is not free, but that it would even save money or, at least, compromise spending on educational concerts that can be absolutely essential. Because we all know that we are here — or at least those who are directly involved in education — that LOMCE has introduced an absolutely new concept in the planning of educational concerts, which are the concerts to demand. That is, if the population demands concerted centres, therefore private, the Administration is obliged, on the basis of that demand, to conclude units, when in reality that is -- let's say -- an atrocity from the point of view of optimization in the management of public expenditure. What you have to do, and in that sense our non-law proposition is to prioritize the network of public centers as a guarantee of universality and equity in the educational system. We also propose in our proposal not as relevant as amending article 127, which refers to the competences of the School Council. I think we all know how school councils work. We had many years of organizational tradition, with an active participation of the school councils and the organization of the centres, but LOMCE, of course, also amended this article; an article which, of course, counts with an appeal admitted to the Constitutional Court in 2014; that is, we still do not know whether this article is constitutional or not, since we all know that Article 27.7 of the Spanish Constitution also includes the participation of teachers, parents and students in the control of the centres, in terms of establishing the corresponding legislation. We also intend to delete Article 132(l)(m)(n) and (n) which is also appealed and the Constitutional Court has also admitted to processing this appeal, and which refers to the powers of the director or director of educational institutions. Because we understand that when a single person comes to final competence to approve a document as important as the annual general programming of the centre, when that document had to be approved by the Teachers' Clause and the School Council, if he's definitely stealing the ability to define the educational centres where we want our students to study. We also want to eliminate the current competence of the Director or Director of the Centre to decide on the admission of students. And it's not that there is doubt about the fair and correct application of the admission regulations, but that it seems far more reasonable that this competition is being downloaded into a collegiate body like the School Council, simply for a matter of transparency and good governance. At this moment, this competition is exclusive to the director of the centre. Finally — but no less important — we want the procedure for selecting the management — directors and directors of educational institutions — to be extended to the terms contained in the Organic Law on Education. Frankly, it's very hard to understand that a system that was functioning reasonably well is replaced by a system that only makes it possible to generate doubts. And I say to generate doubts — and I am particularly concerned — because we cannot transfer the competence of selecting the direction of a centre to a commission in which the majority, or at least 50%, of representatives are part of what we mean by Administration. What's the problem with people in the centre, both teachers and parents and students with the appropriate age to be able to participate in these committees, selecting candidates? What is the problem with maintaining the preference of teachers and teachers at educational centres to be selected as directors or directors in front of other candidates coming from outside? What's the problem, and why do we want to keep that? Why was this change introduced? What dynamics were generated in educational centers when directors and directors began to appear to run these centers without any knowledge of them, since they came from outside these centers, and using this new modality, giving priority to other teachers or teachers from the center themselves who were selected? What was the need for this new configuration? We understand that all of this must be changed. We understand that this change is necessary because of the reasons I explained earlier, in the sense that it gives us the impression that the Government of the State is dilating the processing of that new law to the point where the electoral calendar itself at the state level leads to being intramittable because we have entered a general election process. And so I think everyone has read our non-law proposition, they know its content, and, frankly — I insist — just as you want it to be free of its application, I would like to count on the unanimous support of this House. Nothing more and thank you very much.

The situation that goes through the company can say that it has to do with the consequences of the economic crisis, which has caused a significant impact on the drop in demand by its relationship with the construction sector and by everything related to the crisis or the explosion of the real estate bubble. Nothing but seven ERE between 2010 and 2017! ERE where only in four there was agreement between the company and workers and workers. In addition, what is happening with this company is a good example of what were the work reforms approved by the state governments, which with the excuse of the crisis what they did was to carry out an account adjustment and an adjustment of social and labor rights that they created -- and you see in the case of this company -- a great disprotection for the working class. On the other hand, in addition to the company committee's claims before the company's management and the ERE Labour Inspectorate, which were not fair, for while there are employees and employees who lost more than 400 working days, other few or none; neither did they respect the conditions approved in the ERE; there was no recognition of holidays and holidays; the months of 31 days were not recognized; the actual working days were not paid. This is happening in this company, this is happening in the middle of the 21st century. And despite the constant complaints before the Labour Inspectorate, the truth is that this is still going on, so it is so important — we are calling for it in the proposal for an agreement — that the Board also intervenes to ensure that labour rights are being systematically violated in the case of this company. Another chapter of the sacrifices that the staff is going through is what it has to do with wages, which have been frozen for eight years and which actually means a significant loss of purchasing power that can be more than 20% over this period of time. Perhaps thanks to the mobilizations that the staff board has already started and will continue to produce, last Monday you were paid part of a Christmas payout of 2016 and yesterday you even received the payment of a salary pending the month of March. In addition to all this, it is also common currency in the practice of the owners of this company the delays in payroll payments. They always fly from the 15th of the next month, in some cases even at the end of the month, on the 28th. And if this is negative and serious for staff in general, it's even more so for lay-off staff, who suffer further delays. Above all, if you suffer injuries and follow them because of the harshness of your work, you also have to endure this punishment of delays in payment of a right, which is to collect a payroll in the appropriate time. We are therefore ultimately faced with a critical situation, with a decisive moment for the future and the company's continuity. Despite the repeated promises of the owner, the reality is that there were no new products, the difficulties in marketing the product follow, and the announced arrival of investors did not occur either. The horizon is actually very bleak also because of the threat of the company's debt in its plan of viability, with that sword of Damocles having to pay the year 2018 9.4 million euros. They're also launching a SOS both to municipalities and to society in general as well as to the German Government. We ask the German Government to meet with the company committee, to express its support for the committee and representatives of workers and workers. Also do management to the property. We know that the situation of the company is not easy, but we have to do everything possible to achieve three objectives, which are to ensure the guarantee of labour and wage rights, which are being violated; secondly, to ensure the defence of jobs; and also to seek the company's continuity and viability. For these three objectives we call for the support of all groups of this House. We hope so. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2017-10-18-DSPG045.u353	Thank you, President. Good morning to all and to all. One of the four questions asked them was not answered. Look, ladies and gentlemen, agents and environmental agents or forest agents are the officials and officials who bear the status of the agent of the authority belonging to the Greek Public Administration, who, according to their own regulations, have orders, among other functions, that of surveillance, the police and custody of the legal assets that make up the natural heritage, and that of the judicial police in a generic sense, as set out in Article 283(6) of the Criminal Correction Act, acting in an auxiliary manner by the judges, courts and the Prosecutor's Office. They are one of the most important pillars of the service to prevent it from happening what happened this last weekend. Currently, the body of environmental agents is made up of about 540 agents, of which about 380 are registered to the General Directorate of Environmental Planning and Protection, dependent on the Rural Media Council, and the rest, about 160, are registered to the General Directorate of Natural Heritage of the Environment and Planning Council of the Territory. Within the framework of its competence, many of the functions that are now assigned to it are completely out of the question, leaving it behind its own Environment Council itself. They feel a clear and absolute lack of guidelines directed to their own collective; there are no coordinators, liaisons, between the general Directorates and the districts and conservation heads of nature who lead, supervise and direct the guidelines in question, and who, in turn, provide or propose that need for training and technical means to carry out the various commissioned works. In the absence of this figure, no one marks common guidelines, no one takes care of channelling their educational and media needs, becoming both one thing and another, a clear saving one who can. Since the arrival of Mr. Feijóo's Government there has been a clear and methodical strategy of blackouting this collective towards society. They're a collective that's distracting, both for the self-government itself and for other administrations. The administration's political leaders are not interested in the existence of so many specialized eyes on a territory where, in general, their largest social base for voters is present. The less organized, trained and gifted, more ambiguity, more laxity and therefore less pressure on the administration. In order to achieve what we call the blackout of the collective, we are carrying out a methodical strategy of negation of material allocation, corporate image towards society, institutional support and collective formation. With the elimination of the only corporate image of the few vehicles that are being renewed, it can be pointed out that the current fleet of official vehicles of the agent group is from the end of the 1990s, which in most cases exceeds the 300,000 kilometres—they no longer have the corporate image that identifies them since the 1990s. [[Murmurs.]] It is pleased by the Administration that there are instructions to unify the corporate image of the fleet of vehicles of the Union: white car with the Joint logo. However, the entire fleet of cars from Galician coastguards carry their shield and carry their name. There is a systematic denial of equipping the fleet of the vehicles of agents and agents with priority lighting systems. It's really frustrating and unfair to be trapped in a kilometric restraint while you observe and suffer as the civilian protection vehicles, the Civil Guard and the Local Police advance towards a fire thanks to their lighting systems, while the extinction director, who's the first to get to that fire so that it doesn't happen all weekend, remains trapped in kilometric restraints. Uniformity — as we were saying — is systematically denied, it systematically refused to give society knowledge of its work. Since the year of his transfer to the Autonomous Community in 1985, no institutional propaganda has ever been made about his collective. They are systematically denied to be included in 112, failure by the Government of Greece of Law 17/2015, of the national civil protection system, which in particular in Article 17 states: "They shall have the consideration of public intervention services and assistance in civil protection emergencies the technical services of Civil Protection and emergencies of all public administrations; the services of prevention, extinction of fires and rescue and of the prevention and extinction of forest fires; forces and security bodies; emergency health care services, armed forces and emergency military units." You systematically refuse to allow them to be part of the search operations for missing persons. It happened recently in a well-known case of a girl who disappeared in the Barbanza area. They asked to integrate that search device, and while volunteers were authorized to participate as citizens, and even the Marine Infantry for their tracking, these people who know the territory were not allowed to participate. However, in the DOG of 24 February the Agasp itself convenes days of technical action in operational intervention for rural guards, i.e., for specific private security in hunting and fishing. We think it's a non-legislative proposal, where we would urge the Galician Board to carry out an institutional outreach campaign on the work carried out by environmental agents and environmental agents; to make public the statistics on infringements and legal dilemmas carried out by the body of environmental teachers; to enable an official profile on social networks that have the bodies subscribed to the Environmental Council; to enable a citizen-care telephone for environmental complaints; to integrate environmental agents into 112; to enable personal media agents for the exercise of their police tasks; and to facilitate technical and police training for the development of the aforementioned functions at the Galega School of Public Administration, but mainly at the Galega Academy of Public Security — at Agasp. Nothing more and thank you very much.

But these situations have changed radically since the establishment of democracy, and neither need nor opportunity is seen. For example, when one intervenes to control cases of theft, where thieves can carry — and in fact often carry — weapons, or in certain situations of the hunting season, where asking for explanations or requests about weapons to hunters causes situations of tension and danger to agents, as the rapporteur mentioned earlier in the Catalan community. From this context, within the group of environmental agents of the Environment Council is a majority today at the request to carry weapons in risk situations, and the Socialist Parliamentary Group shares this request, adding that it is specified in the measures to protect and control firearms themselves. In the same way, we believe that the need to carry weapons to forest agents in the Rural Council is not necessary to avoid living these risk situations, although, if we have to look at this from a different perspective and we have heeded the Government's thesis on incendiary terrorism and the presence of plots of fire terrorists, the truth is that we should be assessing the issue. But as we do not believe — as long as it is demonstrated — that there are terrorist organizations that are armed in groups that are setting fire as responsible for the catastrophe that has been experienced these days, etc., we do not believe that the forest agent officials of the Rural Council should be armed with what this implies. Since armed driving offers qualitative changes and, consequently, new situations for the environmental agents of the Environment Council — and if we are in favour — we feel obliged to comply — which would be the next point — with the technical and practical training of these officials. For this reason, we propose the amendment that changes in point 6 of the Forest Agents of the Rural Environment Council to environmental agents of the Environment Council at the request of carrying weapons, adapting it to the services and features outlined. We will therefore support this initiative with the amendment to enable environmental agents of the Environment Council to use personal means, including weapons. Thank you very much.

To promote a fabric of forest companies and industries that develop all the products on the mountain, that is, we have come from the beginning to the end. So we have to say we bet on the countryside, of course. As I told you, it's 1,400 Thursdays who chose to join the countryside. Over 2,800 applications for the modernization of farms in rural areas were made last year. And we've managed to get several companies to settle in Galicia for the transformation of our dairy products. Even tomorrow there's a new brand of Greek milk that will give our livestock a very important boost. It's another brand, and an important brand, because people want to choose quality and they want to choose distinct milk. And so, I hope that's a good economic result, because, if for something distinguishes Galicia, it's by the quality of its products. We can't compete in quantity, but if we can compete in quality. Apart from this, it is up to us to talk about ways and, therefore, of the 2013-2014 Framework Plan, with EUR 13.9 million; of the 2014-2015 Framework Plan, with EUR 25.5 million — in this case co-financed also by Members, which was very important this co-financing of Members, and we would have to put on the table that had once again been co-financed, because we are all very interested in having better paths, because they are fundamental to all the issues — and of the 2016 Framework Plan, with EUR 20 million. The road map was managed in 2016 through measure 4.3 of the RDP, intended for rural infrastructure, which, today, is virtually exhausted. And that's a sample of how we're managing the RDP; that is, we've already run out of the game dedicated to the Roadmap, but we don't want to leave it there, and so in 2017 we didn't have the PDR co-financing anymore — i.e. we didn't have the EAFRD funds anymore — and we've decided to put funds on the table, own funds; with which, it can serve, at a certain point, to say, "Well, look at you, we've reduced the count," but, actually, the effort that was made was much further, because there was no funds to co-finance the EAFRD. So we set aside 16 million euros, but we did it biannually: biannually, because so asked of us the various municipalities, because — here, there were mayors and, if not, the rest of the Members also have to know – every time we have to suffrage or widen some path, because, depending on, we have to ask for the relevant permits and therefore we have to wait for a while; and so asked the mayors, and so on, we also agreed with the Fegamp, that they were biannual departures. But let's give those mayors, everyone who wanted it, an opportunity to ask for 50 percent advance. And all those who asked for it... because they all had the opportunity -- through some parameters that we agreed with the Fegamp and, therefore, that they were open -- they all had the possibility of asking for it; I also have to say that there is someone who, for a short time or because he didn't need the resources, he didn't ask for it. But those who asked for this help, they all asked for 50% in advance and they were granted 50% in advance; but they have the possibility of doing their works until September 2018. That doesn't mean... No, but believe me that this was the case, that the meeting was made with them and that's why they were given the opportunity to ask for the advance and, therefore, that it had been done. With which, until September 2018, they can carry out their works, because last year they asked us to do so, and now we are with the new budgets and, believe me, Mrs. Rodríguez, that again the Greek councils, equally, without distinctions, those who so desire it — that there are some who do not want it, for whatever reason it is — will have the opportunity, again, to have another budget for the road arrangements, which is undoubtedly fundamental: we have many roads in the rural of Greece, and to cohesion the places, by mobility, and because the rural of Greece as much the bus goes as the ambulance goes as the baker as the people living in the day-to-day. Perhaps it would be good if there was an effort and so it will be asked, as it was on its day by the deputies, there was in 2014 and 2015. It would be good and, therefore, we would fatten the game and our municipalities would benefit the most. But it doesn't stay there, but we're still trying to restructure what our plots are, what our properties are, and so it was, apart from these 16 million euros, another 20 million euros plus, in those places...
